│     • Save the updated model with its updated weights in TorchScript format  │

│  • The TorchScript model at /app/model.pt must be able to load the original  │

│  • The MSE loss using the updated output_layer must be lower than the        │
│    original loss obtained using the unmodified weights from /app/weights.pt. │
│  • You must not modify the /app/weights.pt file                              │

│ #!/usr/bin/env python3 import torch import torch.nn as nn from               │
│ torch.utils.data import TensorDataset, DataLoader                            │

│ def infer_layers_from_state(state: dict): """ Infer an ordered list of       │
│ (name, module) from a flat state_dict. - Keys are expected in the form:      │
│ "<prefix>.<param>" - Supports Linear (2D weight) and Conv2d (4D weight)      │
│ layers. - Preserves the first-seen order of prefixes. """ from collections   │
│ import OrderedDict                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Determine unique prefixes in order of appearance                          │
│  prefixes = []                                                               │
│  for key in state.keys():                                                    │
│      pref = key.split(&#x27;.&#x27;)[0]                                      │
│      if pref not in prefixes:                                                │
│          prefixes.append(pref)                                               │
│                                                                              │
│  layers = []                                                                 │
│  for pref in prefixes:                                                       │
│      w_key = f&#x27;{pref}.weight&#x27;                                      │
│      if w_key not in state:                                                  │
│          # If this prefix doesn&#x27;t have a weight, skip gracefully        │
│          continue                                                            │
│      w = state[w_key]                                                        │
│      bias_key = f&#x27;{pref}.bias&#x27;                                     │
│      has_bias = bias_key in state                                            │
│                                                                              │
│      if w.dim() == 2:                                                        │
│          # Linear: [out_features, in_features]                               │
│          out_features, in_features = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1])        │
│          linear = nn.Linear(in_features, out_features, bias=has_bias)        │
│          linear.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])                              │
│          if has_bias:                                                        │
│              linear.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])                         │
│          layers.append((pref, linear))                                       │
│      elif w.dim() == 4:                                                      │
│          # Conv2d: [out_channels, in_channels, kH, kW]                       │
│          out_c, in_c, kH, kW = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1]),             │
│  int(w.shape[2]), int(w.shape[3])                                            │
│          conv = nn.Conv2d(in_c, out_c, kernel_size=(kH, kW), bias=has_bias)  │
│          conv.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])                                │
│          if has_bias:                                                        │
│              conv.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])                           │
│          layers.append((pref, conv))                                         │
│      else:                                                                   │
│          # Unsupported dimension                                             │
│          print(f&quot;Warning: Unsupported weight dimension {w.dim()} for    │
│  {pref}, skipping.&quot;)                                                    │
│  return layers                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ class RecoveredModel(nn.Module): def init(self, layers_info): super().init() │
│ self.layers = nn.ModuleList([m for _, m in layers_info]) self.layer_names =  │
│ [name for name, _ in layers_info] self.output_layer = self.layers[-1]        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  def forward(self, x):                                                       │
│      for i, layer in enumerate(self.layers):                                 │
│          x = layer(x)                                                        │
│          # Apply ReLU after all but the last layer if possible               │
│          if i != len(self.layers) - 1:                                       │
│              x = torch.relu(x)                                               │
│      return x                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ def load_weights_into_model(model: RecoveredModel, flat_state: dict): """    │
│ Map flat state_dict keys to corresponding modules in the model. This assigns │
│ weights for Linear and Conv2d modules by matching by the module's "name"     │
│ prefix used during construction. """ assigned = 0 for idx, (name, mod) in    │
│ enumerate(zip(model.layer_names, model.layers)): if isinstance(mod,          │
│ nn.Linear): w_key = f'{name}.weight' if w_key in flat_state: with            │
│ torch.no_grad(): mod.weight.data.copy_(flat_state[w_key]) assigned += 1      │
│ b_key = f'{name}.bias' if b_key in flat_state and mod.bias is not None: with │
│ torch.no_grad(): mod.bias.data.copy_(flat_state[b_key]) assigned += 1 elif   │
│ isinstance(mod, nn.Conv2d): w_key = f'{name}.weight' if w_key in flat_state: │
│ with torch.no_grad(): mod.weight.data.copy_(flat_state[w_key]) assigned += 1 │
│ b_key = f'{name}.bias' if b_key in flat_state and mod.bias is not None: with │
│ torch.no_grad(): mod.bias.data.copy_(flat_state[b_key]) assigned += 1 else:  │
│ # Unknown module type; skip pass return assigned                             │
│                                                                              │
│ def main(): # Load weights (state_dict) state = torch.load(WEIGHTS_PATH,     │
│ map_location='cpu') if not isinstance(state, dict): raise                    │
│ SystemExit('Weights file does not contain a dict (state_dict).')             │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Reconstruct architecture from state dict                                  │
│  layers_info = infer_layers_from_state(state)                                │
│  if not layers_info:                                                         │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;Failed to infer any layers from the              │
│  weights.&#x27;)                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  model = RecoveredModel(layers_info)                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  # Load weights into the reconstructed model                                 │
│  loaded = load_weights_into_model(model, state)                              │
│  # If not all layers were assigned, attempt a direct load (strict=False)     │
│  if loaded == 0 or loaded &lt; len(layers_info):                             │
│      try:                                                                    │
│          model.load_state_dict(state, strict=False)                          │
│      except Exception as e:                                                  │
│          print(&#x27;Direct load_state_dict with strict=False                │
│  failed:&#x27;, e)                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  device = torch.device(&#x27;cpu&#x27;)                                      │
│  model.to(device)                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  # Load dataset                                                              │
│  dataset = torch.load(DATASET_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)            │
│  if isinstance(dataset, tuple) and len(dataset) == 2:                        │
│      X, Y = dataset                                                          │
│      X = torch.as_tensor(X)                                                  │
│      Y = torch.as_tensor(Y)                                                  │
│      ds = TensorDataset(X, Y)                                                │
│  elif isinstance(dataset, TensorDataset):                                    │
│      ds = dataset                                                            │
│  else:                                                                       │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;Unsupported dataset format: expected (X, Y)      │
│  tuple or TensorDataset.&#x27;)                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  loader = DataLoader(ds, batch_size=64, shuffle=False)                       │
│                                                                              │
│  # Compute initial MSE                                                       │
│  model.eval()                                                                │
│  criterion = nn.MSELoss()                                                    │
│  with torch.no_grad():                                                       │
│      total_loss = 0.0                                                        │
│      count = 0                                                               │
│      for xb, yb in loader:                                                   │
│          preds = model(xb.to(device))                                        │
│          total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() * xb.size(0)   │
│          count += xb.size(0)                                                 │
│      initial_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0 else                   │
│  float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)                                                      │
│  print(f&#x27;Initial MSE on dataset: {initial_mse}&#x27;)                   │
│                                                                              │
│  # Freeze all layers except the final output_layer                           │
│  for i, mod in enumerate(model.layers):                                      │
│      for p in mod.parameters():                                              │
│          p.requires_grad = (i == len(model.layers) - 1)                      │
│                                                                              │
│  # Train only the output_layer                                               │
│  params_to_opt = [p for p in model.output_layer.parameters() if              │
│  p.requires_grad]                                                            │
│  if len(params_to_opt) == 0:                                                 │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;No parameters available to train in the          │
│  output_layer.&#x27;)                                                        │
│  optimizer = torch.optim.SGD(params_to_opt, lr=0.01, momentum=0.9)           │
│                                                                              │
│  model.train()                                                               │
│  train_epochs = 200                                                          │
│  for epoch in range(train_epochs):                                           │
│      epoch_loss = 0.0                                                        │
│      for xb, yb in loader:                                                   │
│          xb = xb.to(device)                                                  │
│          yb = yb.to(device)                                                  │
│          optimizer.zero_grad()                                               │
│          preds = model(xb)                                                   │
│          loss = criterion(preds, yb)                                         │
│          loss.backward()                                                     │
│          optimizer.step()                                                    │
│          epoch_loss += loss.item() * xb.size(0)                              │
│      if (epoch + 1) % 50 == 0:                                               │
│          avg = epoch_loss / len(ds)                                          │
│          print(f&#x27;Epoch {epoch+1}/{train_epochs}, Training MSE:          │
│  {avg:.6f}&#x27;)                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  # Compute new MSE after tuning                                              │
│  model.eval()                                                                │
│  with torch.no_grad():                                                       │
│      total_loss = 0.0                                                        │
│      count = 0                                                               │
│      for xb, yb in loader:                                                   │
│          preds = model(xb.to(device))                                        │
│          total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() * xb.size(0)   │
│          count += xb.size(0)                                                 │
│      new_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0 else                       │
│  float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)                                                      │
│  print(f&#x27;New MSE after tuning output_layer: {new_mse}&#x27;)            │
│                                                                              │
│  # Save updated model to TorchScript                                         │
│  scripted = torch.jit.script(model)                                          │
│  scripted.save(OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH)                                           │
│  print(f&#x27;Saved TorchScript model to {OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH}&#x27;)         │
│                                                                              │

│ if name == 'main': main()                                                    │

│ Notes and considerations                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • The script reconstructs a naive architecture by interpreting each unique  │
│    prefix in the weights state_dict as a separate layer (Linear when the     │
│    parameter tensor is 2D, Conv2d when 4D). This is commonly how PyTorch     │
│    stores layers in a state dict.                                            │
│  • If the original architecture contains non-standard modules (e.g., custom  │
│    layers, BatchNorm, etc.), this script would need to be extended to handle │
│    those cases. The given approach handles Linear and Conv2d, which cover    │
│    many feed-forward and CNN components.                                     │
│  • The initial load uses a best-effort mapping. If a direct                  │
│    load_state_dict(state, strict=False) is possible, it will apply.          │
│    Otherwise, weights are assigned to matching modules by name.              │
│  • The tuning process freezes all layers except output_layer and trains only │
│    that part. The learning rate and epoch count can be tuned if you observe  │
│    insufficient improvement.                                                 │
│  • The final TorchScript model is written to /app/model.pt. This model       │
│    should load the original weights from /app/weights.pt (since only         │
│    output_layer is updated) and reflect the updated weights for the final    │
│    layer.                                                                    │
│  • Do not modify /app/weights.pt. The script uses it as the source of the    │
│    original weights.                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ If you’d like, I can adapt the script to:                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • Be more robust to additional module types (e.g., BatchNorm, activations,  │
│    or custom layers).                                                        │
│  • Verify a strict equivalence of the saved model’s state_dict to the        │
│    original (apart from output_layer) after saving model.pt, by comparing    │
│    state dictionaries programmatically.                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ Limitations here                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • PyTorch is not installed in this environment, so I cannot execute the     │
│    script to perform the actual training or save the TorchScript model       │
│    within this session.                                                      │
│  • Please run the above script in an environment where PyTorch is available  │
│    to achieve the specified task.                                            │

│ finish_reason='stop' content='I’m adopting the solver persona and providing  │
│ a robust, ready-to-run PyTorch script you can execute in a PyTorch-enabled   │
│ environment. The environment here doesn’t have PyTorch installed, so I can’t │
│ run it here, but this script should meet all your requirements when run on   │
│ your side.\n\nWhat this does\n- Reconstructs the original architecture by    │
│ inferring layers from /app/weights.pt.\n- Creates a RecoveredModel with a    │
│ ModuleDict keyed by the original layer prefixes (e.g., layer1, layer2,       │
│ output_layer) to preserve the exact state_dict keys.\n- Loads the original   │
│ weights from /app/weights.pt into this model.\n- Computes the initial MSE on │
│ the dataset in /app/dataset.pt.\n- Freezes all layers except the final       │
│ output_layer and trains only that layer to reduce MSE.\n- Computes the new   │
│ MSE after tuning.\n- Saves the updated model as a TorchScript file to        │
│ /app/model.pt.\n- Ensures the only difference between /app/model.pt and      │
│ /app/weights.pt should be in the output_layer weights (assuming architecture │
│ reconstruction matches the original).\n\nUsage\n- In a PyTorch-enabled       │
│ environment:\n  - Save the script below as recover_and_tune.py\n  - Run:     │
│ python recover_and_tune.py\n- Validation steps after running:\n  -           │
│ torch.jit.load(&#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;) should succeed\n  - Compare        │
│ state_dicts of /app/weights.pt and the loaded /app/model.pt to verify only   │
│ output_layer differs\n  - The reported initial MSE should be higher than the │
│ MSE after tuning\n  - The script does not modify /app/weights.pt; it only    │
│ writes /app/model.pt\n\nPython script (recover_and_tune.py)\n- Copy this     │
│ into a new file named recover_and_tune.py and run with Python 3 in a         │
│ PyTorch-equipped environment.\n\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport              │
│ torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nfrom torch.utils.data import TensorDataset,    │
│ DataLoader\nimport os\n\nWEIGHTS_PATH =                                      │
│ &#x27;/app/weights.pt&#x27;\nDATASET_PATH =                                  │
│ &#x27;/app/dataset.pt&#x27;\nOUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH =                            │
│ &#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;\n\ndef infer_layers_from_state(state: dict):\n     │
│ """\n    Infer an ordered list of (name, module) from a flat state_dict.\n   │
│ - Keys are expected in the form: "<prefix>.weight" and "<prefix>.bias"\n     │
│ - Supports Linear (2D weight) and Conv2d (4D weight) layers.\n    -          │
│ Preserves the first-seen order of prefixes.\n    """\n    prefixes = []\n    │
│ for key in state.keys():\n        pref = key.split(&#x27;.&#x27;)[0]\n       │
│ if pref not in prefixes:\n            prefixes.append(pref)\n\n    layers =  │
│ []\n    for pref in prefixes:\n        w_key = f&#x27;{pref}.weight&#x27;\n  │
│ if w_key not in state:\n            # If this prefix doesn&#x27;t have a     │
│ weight, skip gracefully\n            continue\n        w = state[w_key]\n    │
│ bias_key = f&#x27;{pref}.bias&#x27;\n        has_bias = bias_key in          │
│ state\n\n        if w.dim() == 2:\n            # Linear: [out_features,      │
│ in_features]\n            out_features, in_features = int(w.shape[0]),       │
│ int(w.shape[1])\n            linear = nn.Linear(in_features, out_features,   │
│ bias=has_bias)\n            linear.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])\n         │
│ if has_bias:\n                linear.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])\n      │
│ layers.append((pref, linear))\n        elif w.dim() == 4:\n            #     │
│ Conv2d: [out_channels, in_channels, kH, kW]\n            out_c, in_c, kH, kW │
│ = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1]), int(w.shape[2]), int(w.shape[3])\n       │
│ conv = nn.Conv2d(in_c, out_c, kernel_size=(kH, kW), bias=has_bias)\n         │
│ conv.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])\n            if has_bias:\n             │
│ conv.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])\n            layers.append((pref,      │
│ conv))\n        else:\n            print(f"Warning: Unsupported weight       │
│ dimension {w.dim()} for {pref}, skipping.")\n    return layers\n\nclass      │
│ RecoveredModel(nn.Module):\n    def init(self, layers_info):\n               │
│ super().init()\n        # layers_info is list of (name, module)\n            │
│ self.layer_keys = [name for name, _ in layers_info]\n        self.layers =   │
│ nn.ModuleDict({name: module for name, module in layers_info})\n        #     │
│ Expose final layer as a separate attribute for easy access (optional)\n      │
│ if self.layer_keys:\n            self.output_layer =                         │
│ self.layers[self.layer_keys[-1]]\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        for   │
│ i, key in enumerate(self.layer_keys):\n            layer =                   │
│ self.layers[key]\n            x = layer(x)\n            # Apply ReLU after   │
│ all but the last layer if possible\n            if i != len(self.layer_keys) │
│ - 1:\n                x = torch.relu(x)\n        return x\n\ndef main():\n   │
│ if not os.path.exists(WEIGHTS_PATH):\n        raise                          │
│ SystemExit(f&#x27;Weights file not found: {WEIGHTS_PATH}&#x27;)\n    if not  │
│ os.path.exists(DATASET_PATH):\n        raise SystemExit(f&#x27;Dataset file  │
│ not found: {DATASET_PATH}&#x27;)\n\n    # Load weights (state_dict)\n        │
│ state = torch.load(WEIGHTS_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)\n    if not   │
│ isinstance(state, dict):\n        raise SystemExit(&#x27;Weights file does   │
│ not contain a dict (state_dict).&#x27;)\n\n    # Reconstruct architecture    │
│ from state dict\n    layers_info = infer_layers_from_state(state)\n    if    │
│ not layers_info:\n        raise SystemExit(&#x27;Failed to infer any layers  │
│ from the weights.&#x27;)\n\n    model = RecoveredModel(layers_info)\n\n    # │
│ Load weights into the reconstructed model (prefer direct mapping)\n          │
│ try:\n        model.load_state_dict(state, strict=False)\n                   │
│ print(&#x27;Loaded weights into model with strict=False.&#x27;)\n    except  │
│ Exception as e:\n        print(&#x27;Direct load_state_dict failed           │
│ with:&#x27;, e)\n\n    device = torch.device(&#x27;cpu&#x27;)\n              │
│ model.to(device)\n\n    # Load dataset\n    dataset =                        │
│ torch.load(DATASET_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)\n    if               │
│ isinstance(dataset, tuple) and len(dataset) == 2:\n        X, Y = dataset\n  │
│ X = torch.as_tensor(X)\n        Y = torch.as_tensor(Y)\n        ds =         │
│ TensorDataset(X, Y)\n    elif isinstance(dataset, TensorDataset):\n          │
│ ds = dataset\n    else:\n        raise SystemExit(&#x27;Unsupported dataset  │
│ format: expected (X, Y) tuple or TensorDataset.&#x27;)\n\n    loader =       │
│ DataLoader(ds, batch_size=64, shuffle=False)\n\n    # Compute initial MSE\n  │
│ model.eval()\n    criterion = nn.MSELoss()\n    with torch.no_grad():\n      │
│ total_loss = 0.0\n        count = 0\n        for xb, yb in loader:\n         │
│ preds = model(xb.to(device))\n            total_loss += criterion(preds,     │
│ yb.to(device)).item() * xb.size(0)\n            count += xb.size(0)\n        │
│ initial_mse = total_loss / count if count > 0 else float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)\n  │
│ print(&#x27;Initial MSE on dataset:&#x27;, initial_mse)\n\n    # Freeze all  │
│ layers except the final output_layer\n    for key in model.layer_keys:\n     │
│ layer = model.layers[key]\n        for p in layer.parameters():\n            │
│ p.requires_grad = False\n    # Enable training for the output_layer\n    if  │
│ model.layer_keys:\n        last_key = model.layer_keys[-1]\n        for p in │
│ model.layers[last_key].parameters():\n            p.requires_grad = True\n   │
│ else:\n        raise SystemExit(&#x27;No layers found in the model to        │
│ train.&#x27;)\n\n    # Prepare optimizer to train only the last layer&#x27;s │
│ parameters\n    params_to_opt = [p for p in                                  │
│ model.layers[model.layer_keys[-1]].parameters() if p.requires_grad]\n    if  │
│ len(params_to_opt) == 0:\n        raise SystemExit(&#x27;No parameters       │
│ available to train in the output_layer.&#x27;)\n    optimizer =              │
│ torch.optim.SGD(params_to_opt, lr=0.01, momentum=0.9)\n\n    # Train\n       │
│ model.train()\n    train_epochs = 200\n    for epoch in                      │
│ range(train_epochs):\n        epoch_loss = 0.0\n        for xb, yb in        │
│ loader:\n            xb = xb.to(device)\n            yb = yb.to(device)\n    │
│ optimizer.zero_grad()\n            preds = model(xb)\n            loss =     │
│ criterion(preds, yb)\n            loss.backward()\n                          │
│ optimizer.step()\n            epoch_loss += loss.item() * xb.size(0)\n       │
│ if (epoch + 1) % 50 == 0:\n            avg = epoch_loss / len(ds)\n          │
│ print(f&#x27;Epoch {epoch+1}/{train_epochs}, Training MSE:                   │
│ {avg:.6f}&#x27;)\n\n    # Compute new MSE after tuning\n    model.eval()\n   │
│ with torch.no_grad():\n        total_loss = 0.0\n        count = 0\n         │
│ for xb, yb in loader:\n            preds = model(xb.to(device))\n            │
│ total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() * xb.size(0)\n          │
│ count += xb.size(0)\n        new_mse = total_loss / count if count > 0 else  │
│ float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)\n    print(&#x27;New MSE after tuning                 │
│ output_layer:&#x27;, new_mse)\n\n    # Save updated model to TorchScript\n   │
│ scripted = torch.jit.script(model)\n    scripted.save(OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH)\n  │
│ print(f&#x27;Saved TorchScript model to {OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH}&#x27;)\n\nif    │
│ name == &#x27;main&#x27;:\n    main()\n\nNotes\n- This approach preserves    │
│ original state_dict keys by using a ModuleDict keyed by the same prefixes    │
│ found in the state dict. This makes load_state_dict(state, strict=False)     │
│ reliable and ensures the only difference after tuning should be in           │
│ output_layer weights.\n- If the original model contains layers beyond Linear │
│ and Conv2d (e.g., BatchNorm, custom modules), you may want to extend the     │
│ infer_layers_from_state function to handle those cases explicitly. The       │
│ script currently handles Linear and Conv2d layers, as these are the most     │
│ common in a weights.pt dict.\n\nNext steps\n- Run in a PyTorch-enabled       │
│ environment.\n- After running, verify:\n  - /app/model.pt loads with         │
│ torch.jit.load\n  - The only differences between /app/weights.pt and the     │
│ loaded /app/model.pt state_dict are in keys under output_layer\n  - The      │
│ final MSE is lower than the initial MSE\n  - /app/weights.pt was not         │
│ modified; only /app/model.pt was created\n\nIf you’d like, I can tailor the  │
│ script further to log exact state-dict diffs or add automated checks for the │
│ difference mask after saving model.pt.'                                      │

│                                                                              │
│ What this does                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Reconstructs the original architecture by inferring layers from           │
│    /app/weights.pt.                                                          │
│  • Creates a RecoveredModel with a ModuleDict keyed by the original layer    │
│    prefixes (e.g., layer1, layer2, output_layer) to preserve the exact       │
│    state_dict keys.                                                          │
│  • Loads the original weights from /app/weights.pt into this model.          │
│  • Computes the initial MSE on the dataset in /app/dataset.pt.               │
│  • Freezes all layers except the final output_layer and trains only that     │
│    layer to reduce MSE.                                                      │
│  • Computes the new MSE after tuning.                                        │
│  • Saves the updated model as a TorchScript file to /app/model.pt.           │
│  • Ensures the only difference between /app/model.pt and /app/weights.pt     │
│    should be in the output_layer weights (assuming architecture              │
│    reconstruction matches the original).                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage                                                                        │
│                                                                              │

│     • Run: python recover_and_tune.py                                        │

│     • torch.jit.load('/app/model.pt') should succeed                         │
│     • Compare state_dicts of /app/weights.pt and the loaded /app/model.pt to │
│       verify only output_layer differs                                       │
│     • The reported initial MSE should be higher than the MSE after tuning    │
│     • The script does not modify /app/weights.pt; it only writes             │
│       /app/model.pt                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ Python script (recover_and_tune.py)                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Copy this into a new file named recover_and_tune.py and run with Python 3 │
│    in a PyTorch-equipped environment.                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ #!/usr/bin/env python3 import torch import torch.nn as nn from               │
│ torch.utils.data import TensorDataset, DataLoader import os                  │
│                                                                              │
│ WEIGHTS_PATH = '/app/weights.pt' DATASET_PATH = '/app/dataset.pt'            │
│ OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH = '/app/model.pt'                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ def infer_layers_from_state(state: dict): """ Infer an ordered list of       │
│ (name, module) from a flat state_dict. - Keys are expected in the form:      │
│ "<prefix>.weight" and "<prefix>.bias" - Supports Linear (2D weight) and      │
│ Conv2d (4D weight) layers. - Preserves the first-seen order of prefixes. """ │
│ prefixes = [] for key in state.keys(): pref = key.split('.')[0] if pref not  │
│ in prefixes: prefixes.append(pref)                                           │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  layers = []                                                                 │
│  for pref in prefixes:                                                       │
│      w_key = f&#x27;{pref}.weight&#x27;                                      │
│      if w_key not in state:                                                  │
│          # If this prefix doesn&#x27;t have a weight, skip gracefully        │
│          continue                                                            │
│      w = state[w_key]                                                        │
│      bias_key = f&#x27;{pref}.bias&#x27;                                     │
│      has_bias = bias_key in state                                            │
│                                                                              │
│      if w.dim() == 2:                                                        │
│          # Linear: [out_features, in_features]                               │
│          out_features, in_features = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1])        │
│          linear = nn.Linear(in_features, out_features, bias=has_bias)        │
│          linear.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])                              │
│          if has_bias:                                                        │
│              linear.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])                         │
│          layers.append((pref, linear))                                       │
│      elif w.dim() == 4:                                                      │
│          # Conv2d: [out_channels, in_channels, kH, kW]                       │
│          out_c, in_c, kH, kW = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1]),             │
│  int(w.shape[2]), int(w.shape[3])                                            │
│          conv = nn.Conv2d(in_c, out_c, kernel_size=(kH, kW), bias=has_bias)  │
│          conv.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])                                │
│          if has_bias:                                                        │
│              conv.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])                           │
│          layers.append((pref, conv))                                         │
│      else:                                                                   │
│          print(f&quot;Warning: Unsupported weight dimension {w.dim()} for    │
│  {pref}, skipping.&quot;)                                                    │
│  return layers                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ class RecoveredModel(nn.Module): def init(self, layers_info): super().init() │
│ # layers_info is list of (name, module) self.layer_keys = [name for name, _  │
│ in layers_info] self.layers = nn.ModuleDict({name: module for name, module   │
│ in layers_info}) # Expose final layer as a separate attribute for easy       │
│ access (optional) if self.layer_keys: self.output_layer =                    │
│ self.layers[self.layer_keys[-1]]                                             │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  def forward(self, x):                                                       │
│      for i, key in enumerate(self.layer_keys):                               │
│          layer = self.layers[key]                                            │
│          x = layer(x)                                                        │
│          # Apply ReLU after all but the last layer if possible               │
│          if i != len(self.layer_keys) - 1:                                   │
│              x = torch.relu(x)                                               │
│      return x                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ def main(): if not os.path.exists(WEIGHTS_PATH): raise SystemExit(f'Weights  │
│ file not found: {WEIGHTS_PATH}') if not os.path.exists(DATASET_PATH): raise  │
│ SystemExit(f'Dataset file not found: {DATASET_PATH}')                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Load weights (state_dict)                                                 │
│  state = torch.load(WEIGHTS_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)              │
│  if not isinstance(state, dict):                                             │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;Weights file does not contain a dict             │
│  (state_dict).&#x27;)                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  # Reconstruct architecture from state dict                                  │
│  layers_info = infer_layers_from_state(state)                                │
│  if not layers_info:                                                         │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;Failed to infer any layers from the              │
│  weights.&#x27;)                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  model = RecoveredModel(layers_info)                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  # Load weights into the reconstructed model (prefer direct mapping)         │
│  try:                                                                        │
│      model.load_state_dict(state, strict=False)                              │
│      print(&#x27;Loaded weights into model with strict=False.&#x27;)         │
│  except Exception as e:                                                      │
│      print(&#x27;Direct load_state_dict failed with:&#x27;, e)               │
│                                                                              │
│  device = torch.device(&#x27;cpu&#x27;)                                      │
│  model.to(device)                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  # Load dataset                                                              │
│  dataset = torch.load(DATASET_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)            │
│  if isinstance(dataset, tuple) and len(dataset) == 2:                        │
│      X, Y = dataset                                                          │
│      X = torch.as_tensor(X)                                                  │
│      Y = torch.as_tensor(Y)                                                  │
│      ds = TensorDataset(X, Y)                                                │
│  elif isinstance(dataset, TensorDataset):                                    │
│      ds = dataset                                                            │
│  else:                                                                       │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;Unsupported dataset format: expected (X, Y)      │
│  tuple or TensorDataset.&#x27;)                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  loader = DataLoader(ds, batch_size=64, shuffle=False)                       │
│                                                                              │
│  # Compute initial MSE                                                       │
│  model.eval()                                                                │
│  criterion = nn.MSELoss()                                                    │
│  with torch.no_grad():                                                       │
│      total_loss = 0.0                                                        │
│      count = 0                                                               │
│      for xb, yb in loader:                                                   │
│          preds = model(xb.to(device))                                        │
│          total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() * xb.size(0)   │
│          count += xb.size(0)                                                 │
│      initial_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0 else                   │
│  float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)                                                      │
│  print(&#x27;Initial MSE on dataset:&#x27;, initial_mse)                     │
│                                                                              │
│  # Freeze all layers except the final output_layer                           │
│  for key in model.layer_keys:                                                │
│      layer = model.layers[key]                                               │
│      for p in layer.parameters():                                            │
│          p.requires_grad = False                                             │
│  # Enable training for the output_layer                                      │
│  if model.layer_keys:                                                        │
│      last_key = model.layer_keys[-1]                                         │
│      for p in model.layers[last_key].parameters():                           │
│          p.requires_grad = True                                              │
│  else:                                                                       │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;No layers found in the model to train.&#x27;)    │
│                                                                              │
│  # Prepare optimizer to train only the last layer&#x27;s parameters          │
│  params_to_opt = [p for p in                                                 │
│  model.layers[model.layer_keys[-1]].parameters() if p.requires_grad]         │
│  if len(params_to_opt) == 0:                                                 │
│      raise SystemExit(&#x27;No parameters available to train in the          │
│  output_layer.&#x27;)                                                        │
│  optimizer = torch.optim.SGD(params_to_opt, lr=0.01, momentum=0.9)           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Train                                                                     │
│  model.train()                                                               │
│  train_epochs = 200                                                          │
│  for epoch in range(train_epochs):                                           │
│      epoch_loss = 0.0                                                        │
│      for xb, yb in loader:                                                   │
│          xb = xb.to(device)                                                  │
│          yb = yb.to(device)                                                  │
│          optimizer.zero_grad()                                               │
│          preds = model(xb)                                                   │
│          loss = criterion(preds, yb)                                         │
│          loss.backward()                                                     │
│          optimizer.step()                                                    │
│          epoch_loss += loss.item() * xb.size(0)                              │
│      if (epoch + 1) % 50 == 0:                                               │
│          avg = epoch_loss / len(ds)                                          │
│          print(f&#x27;Epoch {epoch+1}/{train_epochs}, Training MSE:          │
│  {avg:.6f}&#x27;)                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  # Compute new MSE after tuning                                              │
│  model.eval()                                                                │
│  with torch.no_grad():                                                       │
│      total_loss = 0.0                                                        │
│      count = 0                                                               │
│      for xb, yb in loader:                                                   │
│          preds = model(xb.to(device))                                        │
│          total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() * xb.size(0)   │
│          count += xb.size(0)                                                 │
│      new_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0 else                       │
│  float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)                                                      │
│  print(&#x27;New MSE after tuning output_layer:&#x27;, new_mse)              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Save updated model to TorchScript                                         │
│  scripted = torch.jit.script(model)                                          │
│  scripted.save(OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH)                                           │
│  print(f&#x27;Saved TorchScript model to {OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH}&#x27;)         │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ if name == 'main': main()                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Notes                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • This approach preserves original state_dict keys by using a ModuleDict    │
│    keyed by the same prefixes found in the state dict. This makes            │
│    load_state_dict(state, strict=False) reliable and ensures the only        │
│    difference after tuning should be in output_layer weights.                │
│  • If the original model contains layers beyond Linear and Conv2d (e.g.,     │
│    BatchNorm, custom modules), you may want to extend the                    │
│    infer_layers_from_state function to handle those cases explicitly. The    │
│    script currently handles Linear and Conv2d layers, as these are the most  │
│    common in a weights.pt dict.                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Next steps                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run in a PyTorch-enabled environment.                                     │
│  • After running, verify:                                                    │
│     • /app/model.pt loads with torch.jit.load                                │
│     • The only differences between /app/weights.pt and the loaded            │
│       /app/model.pt state_dict are in keys under output_layer                │
│     • The final MSE is lower than the initial MSE                            │
│     • /app/weights.pt was not modified; only /app/model.pt was created       │
│                                                                              │
│ If you’d like, I can tailor the script further to log exact state-dict diffs │
│ or add automated checks for the difference mask after saving model.pt.       │

╭───────────────────── logging - solver - entire llm_res ──────────────────────╮
│ finish_reason='stop' content='You&#x27;re right to want a robust, explicit   │
│ approach. In this environment PyTorch isn’t installed, so I can’t run or     │
│ validate the run here. Below is a refined, more robust recover_and_tune.py   │
│ script that you can run in a PyTorch-enabled environment. It reconstructs    │
│ the architecture by attaching modules with the exact prefixes from the       │
│ state_dict (e.g., layer1, layer2, output_layer), loads the original weights, │
│ computes initial MSE, freezes all layers except the final one, tunes only    │
│ the output_layer, computes the new MSE, and saves a TorchScript model to     │
│ /app/model.pt. It also includes a straightforward post-hoc check you can run │
│ to verify that only output_layer weights changed.\n\nWhat to run in a        │
│ PyTorch-enabled environment\n- Install PyTorch (example: pip install torch   │
│ torchvision).\n- Save the script below as recover_and_tune.py.\n- Run:       │
│ python recover_and_tune.py\n- Afterward, validate as described in the        │
│ Verification section.\n\nCode: recover_and_tune.py\npython\n#!/usr/bin/env   │

│ &quot;&quot;&quot;\n    Infer an ordered list of (name, module) from a flat  │

│ nn.Conv2d(in_c, out_c, kernel_size=(kH, kW), bias=has_bias)\n                │
│ conv.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])\n            if has_bias:\n             │
│ conv.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])\n            layers.append((pref,      │
│ conv))\n        else:\n            print(f&quot;Warning: Unsupported weight  │
│ dimension {w.dim()} for {pref}, skipping.&quot;)\n    return layers\n\nclass │
│ RecoveredModel(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, layers_info):\n           │
│ &quot;&quot;&quot;\n        Layers_info: list of (name, module). Each        │
│ \&#x27;name\&#x27; is the original prefix\n        (e.g.,                    │
│ \&#x27;layer1\&#x27;, \&#x27;layer2\&#x27;, \&#x27;output_layer\&#x27;) to   │
│ preserve exact state_dict keys.\n        We attach each module as an         │
│ attribute with its original name to ensure\n        state_dict compatibility │
│ (e.g., model.layer1.weight).\n        &quot;&quot;&quot;\n                   │
│ super().__init__()\n        self.layer_names = []\n        # Attach modules  │
│ with original prefixes as attributes\n        for name, mod in               │
│ layers_info:\n            setattr(self, name, mod)\n                         │
│ self.layer_names.append(name)\n        # Convenience alias to the final      │
│ (output) layer\n        if self.layer_names:\n            self.output_layer  │
│ = getattr(self, self.layer_names[-1])\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n         │

│ float(\&#x27;inf\&#x27;)\n    print(\&#x27;Initial MSE on dataset:\&#x27;,   │

│ raise SystemExit(\&#x27;No layers found in the model to train.\&#x27;)\n\n   │

│ count += xb.size(0)\n        new_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0    │

│ print(f\&#x27;Saved TorchScript model to {OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH}\&#x27;)\n\nif  │
│ __name__ == \&#x27;__main__\&#x27;:\n    main()\n\n\nVerification steps you  │

│ python recover_and_tune.py\n- TorchScript load check:\n  - import torch\n  - │
│ m = torch.jit.load(&#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;)\n  - m.eval()\n- State-dict    │
│ difference check (after running and obtaining /app/model.pt):\n  - orig =    │
│ torch.load(&#x27;/app/weights.pt&#x27;, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)\n  -   │
│ ts = torch.jit.load(&#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;,                               │
│ map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)\n  - ts_sd = ts.state_dict()\n  - diffs = [k   │
│ for k in orig.keys() if k in ts_sd and not torch.allclose(orig[k],           │
│ ts_sd[k]).item() if isinstance(orig[k], torch.Tensor)]\n  - Expect diffs to  │
│ be a subset of keys under the final output_layer only (e.g.,                 │
│ &#x27;output_layer.weight&#x27;, &#x27;output_layer.bias&#x27;, or whatever  │
│ your final layer prefixes are).\n- MSE improvement check:\n  - Ensure        │
│ new_mse < initial_mse printed by the script.\n- Non-modified weights         │
│ check:\n  - Confirm that keys other than those under the final output_layer  │
│ are identical between the two state_dicts (you can compute a quick diff and  │
│ assert all equal).\n\nNotes and caveats\n- The provided approach preserves   │
│ original state_dict keys by attaching modules to the exact prefixes found in │
│ the state dict (e.g., layer1, layer2, output_layer). This helps ensure that  │
│ loading /app/model.pt can reconstruct or map weights to the same keys as     │
│ /app/weights.pt, except for any updated output_layer weights.\n- If the      │
│ original model contains non-Linear/Conv2d layers (e.g., BatchNorm, custom    │
│ modules), you may need to extend infer_layers_from_state to handle those     │

│ creating nn.BatchNorm2d accordingly).\n- The script does not modify          │
│ /app/weights.pt. It writes /app/model.pt as a separate file.\n- Ensure that  │

│ script further for additional module types or add an automated post-check    │
│ that asserts the only differences between the two state dicts are within the │
│ output_layer keys, and optionally reports the exact differing                │

│ completion_tokens=5608) cached=False logprobs=None thought=None              │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭────────────────────────────── solver - message ──────────────────────────────╮
│ You're right to want a robust, explicit approach. In this environment        │
│ PyTorch isn’t installed, so I can’t run or validate the run here. Below is a │
│ refined, more robust recover_and_tune.py script that you can run in a        │
│ PyTorch-enabled environment. It reconstructs the architecture by attaching   │
│ modules with the exact prefixes from the state_dict (e.g., layer1, layer2,   │
│ output_layer), loads the original weights, computes initial MSE, freezes all │
│ layers except the final one, tunes only the output_layer, computes the new   │
│ MSE, and saves a TorchScript model to /app/model.pt. It also includes a      │
│ straightforward post-hoc check you can run to verify that only output_layer  │
│ weights changed.                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ What to run in a PyTorch-enabled environment                                 │
│                                                                              │
│  • Install PyTorch (example: pip install torch torchvision).                 │
│  • Save the script below as recover_and_tune.py.                             │
│  • Run: python recover_and_tune.py                                           │
│  • Afterward, validate as described in the Verification section.             │
│                                                                              │
│ Code: recover_and_tune.py                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  #!/usr/bin/env python3                                                      │
│  import torch                                                                │
│  import torch.nn as nn                                                       │
│  from torch.utils.data import TensorDataset, DataLoader                      │
│  import os                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  WEIGHTS_PATH = &#x27;/app/weights.pt&#x27;                                  │
│  DATASET_PATH = &#x27;/app/dataset.pt&#x27;                                  │
│  OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH = &#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;                              │
│                                                                              │
│  def infer_layers_from_state(state: dict):                                   │
│      &quot;&quot;&quot;                                                      │
│      Infer an ordered list of (name, module) from a flat state_dict.         │
│      - Keys are expected in the form: &quot;&lt;prefix&gt;.weight&quot; and  │
│  &quot;&lt;prefix&gt;.bias&quot;                                             │
│      - Supports Linear (2D weight) and Conv2d (4D weight) layers.            │
│      - Preserves the first-seen order of prefixes.                           │
│      &quot;&quot;&quot;                                                      │
│      # Preserve insertion order of prefixes as they appear in the keys       │
│      prefixes = []                                                           │
│      for key in state.keys():                                                │
│          pref = key.split(&#x27;.&#x27;)[0]                                  │
│          if pref not in prefixes:                                            │
│              prefixes.append(pref)                                           │
│                                                                              │
│      layers = []                                                             │
│      for pref in prefixes:                                                   │
│          w_key = f&#x27;{pref}.weight&#x27;                                  │
│          if w_key not in state:                                              │
│              # If this prefix doesn&#x27;t have a weight, skip gracefully    │
│              continue                                                        │
│          w = state[w_key]                                                    │
│          bias_key = f&#x27;{pref}.bias&#x27;                                 │
│          has_bias = bias_key in state                                        │
│                                                                              │
│          if w.dim() == 2:                                                    │
│              # Linear: [out_features, in_features]                           │
│              out_features, in_features = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1])    │
│              linear = nn.Linear(in_features, out_features, bias=has_bias)    │
│              linear.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])                          │
│              if has_bias:                                                    │
│                  linear.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])                     │
│              layers.append((pref, linear))                                   │
│          elif w.dim() == 4:                                                  │
│              # Conv2d: [out_channels, in_channels, kH, kW]                   │
│              out_c, in_c, kH, kW = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1]),         │
│  int(w.shape[2]), int(w.shape[3])                                            │
│              conv = nn.Conv2d(in_c, out_c, kernel_size=(kH, kW),             │
│  bias=has_bias)                                                              │
│              conv.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])                            │
│              if has_bias:                                                    │
│                  conv.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])                       │
│              layers.append((pref, conv))                                     │
│          else:                                                               │
│              print(f&quot;Warning: Unsupported weight dimension {w.dim()}    │
│  for {pref}, skipping.&quot;)                                                │
│      return layers                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  class RecoveredModel(nn.Module):                                            │
│      def __init__(self, layers_info):                                        │
│          &quot;&quot;&quot;                                                  │
│          Layers_info: list of (name, module). Each &#x27;name&#x27; is the   │
│  original prefix                                                             │
│          (e.g., &#x27;layer1&#x27;, &#x27;layer2&#x27;,                      │
│  &#x27;output_layer&#x27;) to preserve exact state_dict keys.                │
│          We attach each module as an attribute with its original name to     │
│  ensure                                                                      │
│          state_dict compatibility (e.g., model.layer1.weight).               │
│          &quot;&quot;&quot;                                                  │
│          super().__init__()                                                  │
│          self.layer_names = []                                               │
│          # Attach modules with original prefixes as attributes               │
│          for name, mod in layers_info:                                       │
│              setattr(self, name, mod)                                        │
│              self.layer_names.append(name)                                   │
│          # Convenience alias to the final (output) layer                     │
│          if self.layer_names:                                                │
│              self.output_layer = getattr(self, self.layer_names[-1])         │
│                                                                              │
│      def forward(self, x):                                                   │
│          for i, name in enumerate(self.layer_names):                         │
│              layer = getattr(self, name)                                     │
│              x = layer(x)                                                    │
│              # Apply ReLU after all but the last layer if possible           │
│              if i != len(self.layer_names) - 1:                              │
│                  x = torch.relu(x)                                           │
│          return x                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  def main():                                                                 │
│      if not os.path.exists(WEIGHTS_PATH):                                    │
│          raise SystemExit(f&#x27;Weights file not found:                     │
│  {WEIGHTS_PATH}&#x27;)                                                       │
│      if not os.path.exists(DATASET_PATH):                                    │
│          raise SystemExit(f&#x27;Dataset file not found:                     │
│  {DATASET_PATH}&#x27;)                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│      # Load weights (state_dict)                                             │
│      state = torch.load(WEIGHTS_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)          │
│      if not isinstance(state, dict):                                         │
│          raise SystemExit(&#x27;Weights file does not contain a dict         │
│  (state_dict).&#x27;)                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│      # Reconstruct architecture from state dict                              │
│      layers_info = infer_layers_from_state(state)                            │
│      if not layers_info:                                                     │
│          raise SystemExit(&#x27;Failed to infer any layers from the          │
│  weights.&#x27;)                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│      model = RecoveredModel(layers_info)                                     │
│                                                                              │
│      # Load weights into the reconstructed model                             │
│      try:                                                                    │
│          model.load_state_dict(state, strict=False)                          │
│          print(&#x27;Loaded weights into model with strict=False.&#x27;)     │
│      except Exception as e:                                                  │
│          print(&#x27;Direct load_state_dict failed with:&#x27;, e)           │
│                                                                              │
│      device = torch.device(&#x27;cpu&#x27;)                                  │
│      model.to(device)                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│      # Load dataset                                                          │
│      dataset = torch.load(DATASET_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)        │
│      if isinstance(dataset, tuple) and len(dataset) == 2:                    │
│          X, Y = dataset                                                      │
│          X = torch.as_tensor(X)                                              │
│          Y = torch.as_tensor(Y)                                              │
│          ds = TensorDataset(X, Y)                                            │
│      elif isinstance(dataset, TensorDataset):                                │
│          ds = dataset                                                        │
│      else:                                                                   │
│          raise SystemExit(&#x27;Unsupported dataset format: expected (X, Y)  │
│  tuple or TensorDataset.&#x27;)                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      loader = DataLoader(ds, batch_size=64, shuffle=False)                   │
│                                                                              │
│      # Compute initial MSE                                                   │
│      model.eval()                                                            │
│      criterion = nn.MSELoss()                                                │
│      with torch.no_grad():                                                   │
│          total_loss = 0.0                                                    │
│          count = 0                                                           │
│          for xb, yb in loader:                                               │
│              preds = model(xb.to(device))                                    │
│              total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() *          │
│  xb.size(0)                                                                  │
│              count += xb.size(0)                                             │
│          initial_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0 else               │
│  float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)                                                      │
│      print(&#x27;Initial MSE on dataset:&#x27;, initial_mse)                 │
│                                                                              │
│      # Freeze all layers except the final output_layer                       │
│      for key in model.layer_names:                                           │
│          layer = getattr(model, key)                                         │
│          for p in layer.parameters():                                        │
│              p.requires_grad = False                                         │
│      # Enable training for the output_layer                                  │
│      if model.layer_names:                                                   │
│          last_key = model.layer_names[-1]                                    │
│          for p in getattr(model, last_key).parameters():                     │
│              p.requires_grad = True                                          │
│      else:                                                                   │
│          raise SystemExit(&#x27;No layers found in the model to              │
│  train.&#x27;)                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│      # Prepare optimizer to train only the last layer&#x27;s parameters      │
│      last_layer = getattr(model, model.layer_names[-1])                      │
│      params_to_opt = [p for p in last_layer.parameters() if                  │
│  p.requires_grad]                                                            │
│      if len(params_to_opt) == 0:                                             │
│          raise SystemExit(&#x27;No parameters available to train in the      │
│  output_layer.&#x27;)                                                        │
│      optimizer = torch.optim.SGD(params_to_opt, lr=0.01, momentum=0.9)       │
│                                                                              │
│      # Train                                                                 │
│      model.train()                                                           │
│      train_epochs = 200                                                      │
│      for epoch in range(train_epochs):                                       │
│          epoch_loss = 0.0                                                    │
│          for xb, yb in loader:                                               │
│              xb = xb.to(device)                                              │
│              yb = yb.to(device)                                              │
│              optimizer.zero_grad()                                           │
│              preds = model(xb)                                               │
│              loss = criterion(preds, yb)                                     │
│              loss.backward()                                                 │
│              optimizer.step()                                                │
│              epoch_loss += loss.item() * xb.size(0)                          │
│          if (epoch + 1) % 50 == 0:                                           │
│              avg = epoch_loss / len(ds)                                      │
│              print(f&#x27;Epoch {epoch+1}/{train_epochs}, Training MSE:      │
│  {avg:.6f}&#x27;)                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│      # Compute new MSE after tuning                                          │
│      model.eval()                                                            │
│      with torch.no_grad():                                                   │
│          total_loss = 0.0                                                    │
│          count = 0                                                           │
│          for xb, yb in loader:                                               │
│              preds = model(xb.to(device))                                    │
│              total_loss += criterion(preds, yb.to(device)).item() *          │
│  xb.size(0)                                                                  │
│              count += xb.size(0)                                             │
│          new_mse = total_loss / count if count &gt; 0 else                   │
│  float(&#x27;inf&#x27;)                                                      │
│      print(&#x27;New MSE after tuning output_layer:&#x27;, new_mse)          │
│                                                                              │
│      # Save updated model to TorchScript                                     │
│      scripted = torch.jit.script(model)                                      │
│      scripted.save(OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH)                                       │
│      print(f&#x27;Saved TorchScript model to {OUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH}&#x27;)     │
│                                                                              │
│  if __name__ == &#x27;__main__&#x27;:                                        │
│      main()                                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │

│ Notes and caveats                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  • The provided approach preserves original state_dict keys by attaching     │

│  • If the original model contains non-Linear/Conv2d layers (e.g., BatchNorm, │

│    GPU-enabled PyTorch install) for training the output_layer.               │

│ If you want, I can tailor the script further for additional module types or  │

│ finish_reason='stop' content='Acknowledged. I can’t run PyTorch here, but    │
│ I’ll give you a compact, robust plan and ready-to-run snippets you can       │
│ execute in a PyTorch-enabled environment. This should let you reconstruct    │
│ the model, evaluate initial MSE, fine-tune only the output_layer, and save   │
│ TorchScript at /app/model.pt with the required verification steps.\n\nPlan   │
│ (concise)\n- Reconstruct architecture from /app/weights.pt by parsing the    │
│ state_dict keys and creating a deterministic sequence of layers (Linear for  │
│ 2D weights, Conv2d for 4D weights) in the same order as prefixes appear.     │
│ Attach each module to the model with its original prefix name (e.g., layer1, │
│ layer2, output_layer) so the state_dict keys align.\n- Load the original     │
│ weights into the reconstructed model (prefer model.load_state_dict(state,    │
│ strict=False)).\n- Load the dataset from /app/dataset.pt and compute the     │
│ initial MSE on the dataset.\n- Freeze all layers except the last             │
│ (output_layer). Train only the output_layer with a small learning rate and a │
│ modest number of epochs.\n- Recompute MSE on the same dataset after          │
│ tuning.\n- Save the updated model to TorchScript at /app/model.pt.\n-        │
│ Verify:\n  - torch.jit.load(&#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;) succeeds.\n  - The    │
│ state_dict of the loaded TorchScript model differs from /app/weights.pt only │
│ in the output_layer keys.\n  - The final MSE is lower than the initial       │
│ MSE.\n  - /app/weights.pt was not modified.\n\nWhat you’ll need\n- A         │
│ PyTorch-enabled environment (pip install torch torchvision                   │
│ torchaudio).\n\nReady-to-run code snippets (copy-paste into a                │
│ PyTorch-enabled env)\n\n1) Reconstruct, load, evaluate, fine-tune, and       │
│ save\n- Save as recover_and_tune.py and run: python                          │
│ recover_and_tune.py\n\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn │
│ as nn\nfrom torch.utils.data import TensorDataset, DataLoader\nimport        │
│ os\n\nWEIGHTS_PATH = &#x27;/app/weights.pt&#x27;\nDATASET_PATH =             │
│ &#x27;/app/dataset.pt&#x27;\nOUTPUT_SCRIPT_PATH =                            │
│ &#x27;/app/model.pt&#x27;\n\ndef infer_layers_from_state(state: dict):\n     │
│ prefixes = []\n    for key in state.keys():\n        pref =                  │
│ key.split(&#x27;.&#x27;)[0]\n        if pref not in prefixes:\n              │
│ prefixes.append(pref)\n    layers = []\n    for pref in prefixes:\n          │
│ w_key = f&#x27;{pref}.weight&#x27;\n        if w_key not in state:\n         │
│ continue\n        w = state[w_key]\n        bias_key =                       │
│ f&#x27;{pref}.bias&#x27;\n        has_bias = bias_key in state\n        if   │
│ w.dim() == 2:\n            out_features, in_features = int(w.shape[0]),      │
│ int(w.shape[1])\n            linear = nn.Linear(in_features, out_features,   │
│ bias=has_bias)\n            linear.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])\n         │
│ if has_bias:\n                linear.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])\n      │
│ layers.append((pref, linear))\n        elif w.dim() == 4:\n                  │
│ out_c, in_c, kH, kW = int(w.shape[0]), int(w.shape[1]), int(w.shape[2]),     │
│ int(w.shape[3])\n            conv = nn.Conv2d(in_c, out_c, kernel_size=(kH,  │
│ kW), bias=has_bias)\n            conv.weight.data.copy_(state[w_key])\n      │
│ if has_bias:\n                conv.bias.data.copy_(state[bias_key])\n        │
│ layers.append((pref, conv))\n        else:\n            print(f"Warning:     │
│ Unsupported weight dimension {w.dim()} for {pref}, skipping.")\n    return   │
│ layers\n\nclass RecoveredModel(nn.Module):\n    def init(self,               │
│ layers_info):\n        super().init()\n        self.layer_names = [name for  │
│ name, _ in layers_info]\n        # Attach modules with their original        │
│ prefixes\n        for name, mod in layers_info:\n            setattr(self,   │
│ name, mod)\n        self.output_layer = getattr(self,                        │
│ self.layer_names[-1])\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        for i, name in   │
│ enumerate(self.layer_names):\n            layer = getattr(self, name)\n      │
│ x = layer(x)\n            if i != len(self.layer_names) - 1:\n               │
│ x = torch.relu(x)\n        return x\n\ndef main():\n    if not               │
│ os.path.exists(WEIGHTS_PATH) or not os.path.exists(DATASET_PATH):\n          │
│ raise SystemExit(&#x27;Weights or dataset file not found.&#x27;)\n\n         │
│ state = torch.load(WEIGHTS_PATH, map_location=&#x27;cpu&#x27;)\n             │
│ layers_info = infer_layers_from_state(state)\n    if not layers_info:\n      │
│ raise SystemExit(&#x27;Failed to infer layers from weights.&#x27;)\n\n       │
│ model = RecoveredModel(layers_info)\n    try:\n                              │
│ model.load_state_dict(state, strict=False)\n        print(&#x27;Loaded       │
│ weights with strict=False.&#x27;)\n    except Exception as e:\n              │
